| CF-B27-L2 | Boundary matrix: CF-B27 | Protected family contract: \| CF-B27-* \| **IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-08 (HB-127/HB-130), REAL-PATH REPAIRED 2026-08-07 (HB-132)** — `tests/unit/cf-b27/`, `tests/hermetic/cf-j21/`. campaign authorization ↔ report across success/timeout/partial/retry/duplicate/stale/skew: valid envelope accepted; every §1 preflight defect refused pre-mutation; ceiling exhaustion mid-scenario preserved as `incomplete`; torn report rejected; report claiming `complete` with a killed scenario or missing grader run refused; exact matrix/installed identity/spend/all axes/gap list/preview mandatory; scenario-scoped mirror graders and fixed-role activation pinned; resumption under a changed config refused; two campaigns cannot share a report identity \| 1/2 \| refusal+evid+state \| E1/E3 \| | B-27 | SOURCE-BOUNDARY-MAP | per-commit | L2 | refusal+evid+state | E1/E3 | behavior | NC-CF-B27-L2 | B-27#**Preflight failure modes — these refuse BEFORE the campaign starts, never mid-run.** Discovering any of them at the first turn wastes the envelope, and three of them invalidate every score the campaign would have produced: - `pnpm install:packaged --replace-source-links` unrun, skipped, stale relative to the campaign's commit pin, or exited non-zero (`CORMIDIA-INV-ACC-7b`). The campaign **asserts that script's exit status**; it does not reimplement the check. - `effort: max` on any harness but `claude` or `opencode` ([`src/runtime/assignment.ts:82`](../src/runtime/assignment.ts:82) admits it nowhere else), which otherwise throws at the first turn. - an app arm whose planner, builder **and** reviewer are one provider family — the builder ≠ reviewer cross-provider collapse AGENTS.md → Working rules forbids outright ([`roles.yaml:52`](../roles.yaml:52) calls it "the single most important pairing in this file"). - an `adaptive_assignments` candidate with no real ratified `qualification_ref` and no **uncertified disclosure** field. Configuration loading validates the reference's *type* without dereferencing it ([`docs/org/apps.md:104`](../docs/org/apps.md:104)), so a plausible-looking string passes — the disclosure field exists to make the omission structurally impossible rather than a matter of diligence. - the authorized commit ≠ checked-out HEAD, the ordered exact eight-file `validation-design/model/` path/digest set is incomplete or untracked, or `docs/qualification/host-policy.yaml` bytes do not match that commit (`assertCampaignRepositoryBinding`); a scenario repository resolving to this repository (`CORMIDIA-INV-ACC-3`). - unattended execution declared with no plan-gate policy — refuse. A declared `auto-continue` policy is legal after F-PT-030's 2026-08-07 ratification only when it applies the ratified rubric criteria and records the resolution durably before build-arm spend., B-27#**Run/report failure modes:** ceiling exhausted mid-scenario (→ `incomplete`, partial evidence preserved); torn report write; a report claiming `complete` while a scenario was killed or a grader run is missing; a scenario silently absent from the report; the matrix actually used missing from a score (a poor outcome then cannot be attributed between "Cormidia is broken" and "that builder was wrong for this"); installed version or tarball identity absent, so "which bytes did this exercise" is unanswerable; resumption under a changed config; two campaigns writing one report identity. | bikramgupta | HB-127-L2 | IMPLEMENTABLE | tests/hermetic/cf-b27-durable/cf-b27-report-store.test.ts, tests/unit/cf-b27/cf-b27-arm-failure.test.ts, tests/unit/cf-b27/cf-b27-org-roles.test.ts, tests/unit/cf-b27/cf-b27-provisioning-git-auth.test.ts, tests/unit/cf-b27/cf-b27-report-shape.test.ts, tests/unit/cf-b27/cf-b27-spend.test.ts, tests/hermetic/cf-b27-durable/cf-b27-report-store-boundary.test.ts |

| CF-OPS-ABUSE | Operational-obligation matrix: CF-OPS-ABUSE | Three-stage L5 abuse assurance: HB-072 creates validation-design/threat-model.md from the checked template; one human authors its ten surfaces and a distinct independent human reviews it. validation-design/threat-model-status.yaml must name artifact: threat-model.md and durably record author, reviewer, authored_at, reviewed_at, human_authored_reviewed, the artifact SHA-256 digest, and all required surface/case metadata. The landed deterministic admission gate rejects awaiting_human_author, digest or path drift, missing reviewed fields, and author/reviewer identity overlap. After admission, a checked-model revision marks HB-072 landed and CF-OPS-ABUSE-THREAT-MODEL evidence complete, then may change HB-073 to pending while binding the admitted digest. Only pending HB-073 may derive one behavior-falsifying abuse case and red-capable detector per admitted surface and record their case, control, test/evidence, result, and exact threat-model digest in validation-design/abuse-case-status.yaml. The threat-model status artifact cannot satisfy HB-073 by itself, and blocked never transitions directly to execution or landed. The interim floor remains CF-INV-001/002/011/015 adversarial coverage, and none of this work is inside RQ-1. | OP-ABUSE | SOURCE-RISK | scheduled | L5 | mixed | E1 | behavior | NC-CF-OPS-ABUSE | — | bikramgupta | HB-073 | IMPLEMENTABLE | EVIDENCE:unobserved:validation-design/abuse-case-status.yaml |

That was wrong and is corrected here rather than diverged from silently. Predecessor/orphan recovery was a REVISION-CHAIN mechanism: coverage records carried `revision`/`predecessor_digest` and a `revision-<n>.json` history directory because `--revise` minted a new decomposition version, and orphan recovery repaired a crash between writing revision n+1 and swapping the current pointer. With `--revise` and the coverage record pruned there are no revisions, so the mechanism has nothing to recover and is PRUNED with them. What genuinely survives is the prepared→completed publication barrier, which is the leg that prevents duplicate GitHub issues after a crash, and it is listed above --> \| 1/2 \| state+refusal+evid+det \| REG \| | OP-REGRESSION | SOURCE-ROUTING | per-commit | L1 | state+refusal+evid+det | REG | behavior | NC-CF-REG-374 | — | bikramgupta | HB-139 | IMPLEMENTABLE | tests/hermetic/cf-reg-374/cli-surface.test.ts, tests/hermetic/cf-reg-374/stateful-decomposition.test.ts |

| CF-REG-382 | Defect-sourced regression families: CF-REG-382 | Protected family contract: \| CF-REG-382 \| #382 — the critical-ops gate had NO `gh repo` subcommand handling at all, so `gh repo create <slug> --private --source … --push` (the exact command `src/org/new-app-guide.ts` emitted for operators), `gh repo delete`, `gh repo edit --visibility public`, and `gh repo archive` every one classified **routine**, while the raw-API spelling of the first (`gh api --method POST /user/repos`) classified human-only through `gh-api-unrecognized` — an effect reachable two ways was governed one way, because `gh api` has a fail-closed default for unrecognized endpoints and the `gh` subcommand surface had none; the same register also missed `cormidia org publish` (#388) and the `--execute` mode of `cormidia app product-docs` (#389), both of which open draft pull requests while their older sibling `cormidia bootstrap publish` was already `outbound-message`/human-only [owning structure: INV-002 (the gate is TOTAL over critical effects) · CF-INV-002; control point: T-1 (false-negative direction — a real critical effect classified routine is authority damage, system-map §5.2); fix: new `repo-provisioning` class, human-only and never-scopeable by derivation from the tier table, covering the `gh repo` mutating subcommands, the repository-root raw-API endpoints, and `cormidia org\|app provision-repo --execute`, with `ACTION_IDENTITY_VERSION` 7→8 cancelling in-flight grants at landing per the F-PT-023 implementation rule; deliberately NOT folded into `repo-collaboration`, whose budgeted tier the composed gate reaches whenever the target verifies as the app's own configured repository — which a provisioning target IS, so folding them would have made repository creation agent-decidable; spec: `tests/unit/cf-reg-382/repo-provisioning-classification.test.ts` (red-then-green: 19 failures against the pre-fix classifier), plus the CI-found companion defect — `git commit-tree` needs an author identity git resolves from config Cormidia does not own, so a machine with none (a fresh onboarding machine, and the CI runner) would have created the repository on GitHub and THEN failed at the commit; the preflight now refuses locally before any network call, and the detector forces the condition deterministically with `user.useConfigOnly` because `git var` auto-detects `user@hostname` on macOS but not on CI — the exact asymmetry that let it through a green local run; the SECOND CI run then exposed the cause underneath — `publicationGit` snapshotted `process.env` at module load, so the suites' own `GIT_AUTHOR_*` pinning did nothing (ESM hoists imports above module bodies, so a caller setting a variable at the top of its file is already too late), and the substrate now reads the environment at CALL time with the hardened values still layered last; detector `tests/hermetic/cf-reg-382/git-env-call-time.test.ts`, red-then-green — plus `tests/hermetic/cf-reg-382/provisioning-transaction.test.ts`, `…/provisioning-verification.test.ts`, and `…/scaffold-path-agreement.test.ts` for the governed lifecycle this class exists to make safe] \| 1/2 \| refusal+det \| REG \| | OP-REGRESSION | SOURCE-ROUTING | per-commit | L1 | refusal+det | REG | behavior | NC-CF-REG-382 | — | bikramgupta | HB-139 | IMPLEMENTABLE | tests/hermetic/cf-reg-382/git-env-call-time.test.ts, tests/hermetic/cf-reg-382/provisioning-transaction.test.ts, tests/hermetic/cf-reg-382/provisioning-verification.test.ts, tests/hermetic/cf-reg-382/scaffold-path-agreement.test.ts, tests/unit/cf-reg-382/repo-provisioning-classification.test.ts |

| CF-REG-385 | Defect-sourced regression families: CF-REG-385 | Protected family contract: \| CF-REG-385 \| #385 — repository identity was decided by a one-line shape regex (`/^[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+\/[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+$/`) at each surface independently, so the unresolved runbook sentinel `<owner>/YOUR_APP_REPOSITORY` passed, was registered into BOTH `apps.yaml` and `.cormidia/config.yaml`, and was emitted into executable `gh repo create`/`label`/`pr`/`issue` commands by a guide that claims it uses the app's exact identities; the existing-app `OWNER/<app>` fallback was the same class of risk [owning structure: CF-J02-S/R · CF-INV-008 (a surface claiming more than its evidence) · CF-INV-015 (an unresolved value must narrow capability, never widen it) · CF-B01-* (unresolved-placeholder target, appended to the boundary's failure modes in this change) · CF-C-OPLIFE; control point: T-7/T-9; spec: `tests/unit/cf-reg-385/repository-identity.test.ts`, `tests/hermetic/cf-reg-385/onboarding-identity-refusal.test.ts`; one centralized parse-don't-cast rule (`src/runtime/repo-identity.ts`) with a 35-row refusal table over both components, case/separator/whitespace variants, URLs, paths, and the `OWNER/<app>` literal, and a 14-row acceptance table (`github/docs`, `acme/app-repository-scanner`, `owner-operator-tools/repo-insights`) proving it is not a substring ban; | CF-REG-388 | Defect-sourced regression families: CF-REG-388 | Protected family contract: \| CF-REG-388 \| #388 — every committed org-home mutation reported its own terminal claim while the Git remote stayed stale: `cormidia new-app` returned `app-created-and-registered` with `apps.yaml` an unstaged local modification and `origin/<default>` still carrying `apps: {}`, and promote/reset/roles-set/upgrade/learning-publish had the same shape. `bootstrap publish` already implemented the safe pattern for bootstrap-owned paths, but it was not a shared invariant. Fixed by one classification registry (`src/org/committed-org-surfaces.ts`), one preflight + journaled idempotent publication transaction (`git-publication{,-substrate,-journal,-execute}.ts`, `org-home-publication.ts`), typed durability states replacing terminal claims (local_only / recorded_locally / pending_publication / pending_merge / reachable_at_remote), divergence detection in `context`, and `cormidia org publish` as the supported reconcile for an already-dirty org home. | CF-REG-389 | Defect-sourced regression families: CF-REG-389 | Protected family contract: \| CF-REG-389 \| #389 — `app product-docs --execute` wrote the keep/reconcile/remove decision only into the human checkout and reported terminal `recorded`, while live planning — which synchronizes Cormidia's MANAGED checkout from the app remote — still read `disposition: null` and refused with "run `cormidia app product-docs` before automated planning", i.e. the exact command the operator had just run. Write side and read side evaluated different repository views and neither message exposed the publication boundary. Fixed by making the disposition an app-repository transaction through #388's primitive (`src/org/product-doc-publication.ts`: manifest-only owned set for keep/reconcile, manifest PLUS byte-exact placeholders bound into one commit for remove, dedicated branch, draft PR, `--publish` resume that never re-records) and by joining the managed-base manifest with Cormidia's OWN publication journal to distinguish five states (`src/org/product-doc-reachability.ts`: absent / recorded_locally / pending_merge / stale_content / current). Managed-checkout isolation is unchanged — planning still reads no human working-tree file; the journal is state-home data. Existing J-02/J-03 journeys, INV-008/010/013 and the B-01/B-14/B-15 composition; no structural addition [owning structure: CF-J02-S/I/R/RC · CF-J03-R · CF-INV-008 · CF-INV-010 · CF-INV-013 · CF-B01-* · CF-B14-* · CF-B15-* · CF-C-OPLIFE · CF-C-OPPLAN; control point: T-7/T-8/T-9; spec: `tests/hermetic/cf-reg-389/product-doc-publication.test.ts` — real `new-app` scaffolds in real git checkouts with real file:// origins on a non-`main` default branch: the exact E2E reproduction (record, publication does not land, planning names the publication and forbids re-recording), governed publication with the exact decision in a fresh clone while the default branch still says null, awaiting-merge naming and planning proceeding after the merge with no second disposition write, atomic `remove` (manifest + all three placeholders in ONE revision), unrelated app content excluded with the operator's checkout and HEAD byte-identical, seeded push failure and seeded lost-createPR-response both idempotent, the no-remote local-only contract, and an isolation control planting a DIFFERENT decision in the human checkout that planning must not read; `tests/unit/cf-reg-389/product-doc-reachability.test.ts` — the five states with their refusal wording, a detector asserting no recorded/pending refusal ever hands back the `--disposition` command, stale content naming path and both hashes, reachable-wins-over-stale-journal ordering, and negative controls for a missing state home and another app's journal entry. CF-REG-369's two message assertions were re-pointed at the more specific replacements (behavior unchanged, wording tightened).
